<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, August 9, 2016 
 
PRESENT:  Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; Robert Black, Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Vice Chairman; and Jason Whiting, Chairman, were present.  
9:05 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Vice-Chairman Jesse Thompson. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: John Hager, 1909 Ponce Place, White Mountain Lakes, spoke to the Board in regards 
to the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District boat dock policy which was approved by 
the Board July 26, 2016.  He spoke about the survey which was sent to residents of Wh ite Mountain Lakes.  He 
spoke about how the current Advisory Committee has been giving this Board bad advice. He asked that the actions 
and results of the July 26, 2016 meeting be thrown out. Chairman Whiting stated that the Advisory Committee 
was voted in by the people, the Committee went through a public process and made a decision, and brought that 
to the Board of Directors.  He expressed appreciation to Mr. Hager for being here and his involvement.  He 
advised Mr. Hager that staff can be directed to meet with him if he has further questions regarding the legalities. 
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: July 26, 2016  b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 July, 2016  c) 
Constable Reports: Snowflake Precinct #3, April, May and June; and Show Low Precinct #5, July 2016 d) Justice 
Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, July; Winslow Precinct #2, July; Snowflake Precinct #3, July; Kayenta 
Precinct #4, July; Show Low Precinct #5, July; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, July 2016 e) Clerk of Superior 
Court Report July 2016 f) Juvenile Probation Report July 2016 g) Approval of two Back Tax Land Parcels sold 
over the counter in the amount of $1,286.52: APN 403 -06-124A and 403 -07-738 h) Approval of the White 
Mountain Apache Tribe to utilize the Ready Navajo County Alert System i) Approval of Eighteenth Amendment 
to Revolving Credit Agreement & Request for Modification of Commitment Amount  j) Letter to the Arizona 
Department of Transportation delegating signatory authority  to Sheriff KC Clark for all official government 
vehicles k) Agent Change and Acquisition of Control for Lakeside Bell Gas and Mini Mart LLC (Liquor License 
Name Change)  l) Appropriation of County Funds for the Fiscal Year 2016 -17 for Cooperative Extension : 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
PUSH PROGRAM: Presentation to August Wellness Warrior of the month, Susan Rodriquez : Callie Jensen 
recognized Susan Rodriquez as our Wellness Warrior for August. Chairman Whiting  congratulated Ms. 
Rodriquez. He further congratulated Callie Jensen on the upcoming birth of her second child. Supervisor 
Williams congratulated Ms. Rodriquez.  
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Eric Scott reviewed the 25-item Personnel Action 
Report. Vice Chairman Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel Action Report as presented ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of grant agreement REDE -16-01 between Navajo County and the 
Arizona Commerce Authority: Olivia Todd discussed the item indicating that the award is $25,875.52 through 
its Rural Eco nomic Development Enhancement Grant Program. She stated that these funds, along with a 
$10,000.00 donation from Summit Healthcare and USAC Program funds will be used to hire a contractor for 
a broadband planning project.  Chairman Whiting and Supervisor Williams thanked Ms. Todd for her work

<!-- page 2 -->
on this. Supervisor Whitesinger also thanked Ms. Todd and spoke about the current limit of services in rural 
areas. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve Grant Agreement REDE-16-01 between Navajo 
County and th e Arizona Commerce Authority ; motion seconded by Supervisor Black . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. County Manager James Jayne thanked Ms. Todd and also Ken Dewitt and Paul Watson 
for their work on this project, as well.  
SHERIFF: Consideration and possible approval of a lease agreement with the City of Show Low for a building: 
Sheriff KC Clark addressed the Board regarding a lease agreement with the City of Show Low for a building 
which will house regional dispatch, the jail annex, the Sheriff’s Office, and Show Low Police Department . He 
spoke about there being discussion about a public safety building 20 years ago. He spoke about the one -time 
money that has come from the partnerships of renting out bed space.  He spoke about the 50-acre current parcel 
where the jail annex is located that can be used by the County.  He further spoke about the benefit of co-locating, 
including information sharing and taxpayer savings. He expressed appreciation for the leadership behind this 
project.  Supervisor Will iams acknowledged Mayor Seymore, Vice Mayor Leech, Show Low City Council, 
Chief Shelley, the Sheriff, the County Attorney’s Office, and Mr. Jayne for their work and partnership on this 
project.  He spoke about the long-term benefit of this project.  He further spoke about the Sheriff’s Office and the 
police department partnering together , which is an efficient and effective way to do government. Supervisor 
Williams made a motion to approve a lease agreement with the City of Show Low for a building ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Whitesinger stated that 
she has always appreciated the leadership of Sheriff Clark and his forward thinking about better utilization of 
resources. Chairman Whiting spoke about the innovative leadership and vision that we have in Navajo County 
and thanked Sheriff Clark . Mr. Jayne thanked Sheriff Clark for his leadership .  He also thanked Bryan Layton 
and Brandt Clark for their work on this project  and how this project is a great example of doing government 
differently.  
SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS/ ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES : Consideration and possible 
adoption of the fiscal year 2016 -17 property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within Navajo 
County: Jalyn Gerlich presented the school tax rates for all applicable governmental entities and indicated that all 
of the superintendents were in agreement with the rates. Supervisor Williams made a motion to adopt the fiscal 
year 2016-17 property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within Navajo County; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. James Menlove discussed the property tax 
rates and the tax rates for o ther governmental entities with in Navajo County. Chairman Whiting thanked Ms. 
Gerlich, Mr. Menlove and Ms. Todd for the ir work. Chairman Whiting indicated to the public that all Special 
Taxing Districts approve their own tax rates before they come to the Board.  
TREASURER: Annual Report of collections, delinquencies and settlement of accounts: Debra Kester reviewed 
the annual report of collections, delinquencies and settlement of accounts. She spoke of the authorization to invest 
for the County. She also reported on the Treasurer ’s processing fund. There were no ques tions from the Board. 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve the acceptance of the annual report of collections, delinquencies 
and settlement of accounts; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
INFORMATION TECHNOLO GY: Consideration and possible award of CDBG #123 -16 bid for 911 
Equipment Upgrade to Durham Communications Inc: Ken Dewitt addressed the Board regarding the award of 
the bid for 911 equipment upgrade to Durham. He thanked Sheriff Clark and his team and Public Works and the 
radio team partnering with the IT team for their work on this project. He spoke about the Northeast Arizona 
Regional Dispatch Center which will combine in the funding for upgrading the dispatch equipment.  Supervisor 
Williams asked if this is the equipment for the Northeast Arizona Regional Dispatch Center. Ken Dewitt indicated

<!-- page 3 -->
that this equipment is for the Regional Dispatch Center in the Holbrook office. County Manager Jayne thanked 
Mr. Dewitt, Sheriff Clark and Paul Watson for t heir work on this project and the Northeast Arizona Regional 
Dispatch Center. Mr. Jayne indicated that the Board made a decision to accept the CDBG allotment last year and 
that there was a lot of work that went into having those CDBG funds eligible for this purpose, to enhance public 
safety in partnership with the other communities throughout the County in the Northeast Arizona Regional 
Dispatch Center model . He spoke about the need to replace the communication equipment in the Holbrook 
Dispatch Center. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to award the CDBG #123 -16 bid for 911 Equipment 
Upgrade to Durham Communications Inc .; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT: Board of 
Directors Session:   Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter  into the White Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation Improvement District Board of Directors Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson.  Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
a. CONSENT A GENDA:  Melissa Buckley spoke about the items in the consent agenda.  Supervisor 
Thompson made a motion to approve the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Black.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
i) Acceptance of the resignations of Norma Loeffler and Roy Loeffler 
ii) Letter to Bank of the West requesting a change in signatories 
b. Consideration and possible award of bid to Cunningham CPA for a third party audit: Melissa Buckley stated 
that on June 22nd, 2016, the Advisory Committee voted to accept the Cunningham bid  at $4,000.00, as long 
as the added expenses did not exceed 25 percent .  Ms. Buckley indicated that Mr. Cunningham ensured that 
the added expenses would not exceed 25% , work can start on August 15 , and the report would be available 
no later than September 30th.  Chairman Whiting thanked Ms. Buckley and Brandt Clark and everyone else 
who has spent a considerable amount of time they have spent working with the Advisory Committee of White 
Mountain Lakes.  Supervisor Williams made a motion to award the bid to Cunningham CPA for a third 
party audit; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson.  
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
COUNTY SUPERVISORS ASSOCIATION:  Presentation of the activities of the County Supervisors 
Association, including a discussion of the recent legislative session : Craig Sullivan , County Supervisors 
Association, gave a presentation of the activities of the County Supervisors Association. He spoke of the recent 
legislative session. He thanked Chairman Whiting for taking the time to meet with lawmakers and participate 
on the Legislative Policy Committee.  He thanked James Jayne for his leadership.  He spoke about the net relief 
that was able to take place for Navajo County in the amount of $470,027.00. Supervisor Thompson thanked Mr. 
Sullivan for his presentation and for representing the coun ties. Supervisor Williams thanked Mr. Sullivan and 
his team for the work that they do for all counties to work together and support each other. Chairman Whiting 
also thanked Mr. Sullivan and his team for their support and keeping us informed. Brad Carlyon asked about the 
long-term county financial structure. Mr. Sullivan indicated that his team is working to gather that information to 
help all counties. Mr. Carlyon further asked about phasing out the flexibility language. Mr. Sullivan indicated that 
we are going to have to defend that every year going forward. County Manager Jayne spoke about the State budget 
and the shifts from counties.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about attending a meeting with Danny Shurter and Glenn Kephart with the White

<!-- page 4 -->
Mountain Apache Tribe, San Carlos Tribe, Arizona Department of Transportation in looking at the development 
of a plan over several years regarding 911, addressing for hous ing, and transportation in the community. She 
spoke about Sunrise Ski Resort and the need for it to continue to thrive.  She spoke about Snow Bowl’s new 
owner who is aggressively marketing and the need for Sunrise Ski Resort  to be a competitor . She spoke a bout 
meetings within the community and leadership meetings that she has attended. Supervisor Black spoke about 
combining a couple of polling places in Shonto which will make it easier for voters . He thanked the Board and 
County for the opportunity to atten d NACo Conference. He spoke about Supervisor Thompson’s work on the 
school bus route initiatives. He spoke of the social issues that were talked about at the conference and the issues 
that governments are faced with. He stated that he is looking forward to  meeting with the Chapters in Pinon 
regarding the gravel project and the Jeddito initiative regarding addressing the roads before the winter comes . 
Supervisor Williams spoke about a lunch this past week with Ben Peterson, a three-time Olympian for wrestling, 
who was in the area .  He spoke about two college -aged wrestlers  from the United States who are currently 
competing at the Olympics. He thanked Glenn Kephart for attending several property owners’ association 
meetings with him in the communities. He spok e about the H eber-Overgaard Chamber meeting tonight  to 
introduce Mr. Kephart. He spoke about the candidate forum and thanked the Navajo County Bar Association for 
putting that on. He expressed his excitement about the Northeast Arizona Regional Dispatch Center and the joint 
building that was approved today. Supervisor Thompson spoke about some of the communities in his area that 
were running low in their water supply. He expressed appreciation for the opportunity to attend the NACo 
Conference.  He further spoke about the roads on the Navajo Reservation. He spoke about his appointment to the 
NACOG Economic Development Committee.  He further spoke about going to Dilkon to honor and appreciate 
Woody Benally who had retired, who expressed appreciation for the partnership with Navajo County.  Supervisor 
Whiting stated that with school getting ready to start, our hearts and prayers are with the teachers, as well as the 
students. He spoke about football and the start of the new  season and how he enjoys working with the youth in 
this area. He thanked Paul Watson for his work on REAL Az and the work on looking at data for our region. He 
spoke about Navajo County being a finalist for some work that could come to Navajo County. He t hanked 
everyone for their efforts and the support of the Board.  
ADJOURN: 10:51 a.m. Vice Chairman Thompson made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
APPROVED:       DATED:  August 23, 2016 
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
